{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}
such that, for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}-1
, any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}
, any
j
,

\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}}\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\in U_{j}}\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\leq 1+\varepsilon\,.

(D.33)

Lemma D.3
.

For
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}\in(0,1)
, for any
\varepsilon>0
and any compact set
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\subset{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta}
, any set
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}\subset{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}
,
it holds

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}\ex_{#1}\left[\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right)\right]\right]

(D.34)

\displaystyle\leq 2\log\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{B}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}},\varepsilon,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})\right)+6{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\varepsilon+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\right)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}

(D.35)

\displaystyle+\ex_{#1}\left[\one\left\{\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}}\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}\geq 1\right\}\cdot\log\left(1+{\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}}\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}}\right)\right]

(D.36)

\displaystyle+\ex_{#1}\left[\one\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\right\}\cdot\log\left({\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta}}\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}}\right)\right]\,.

(D.37)

Proof.

First, let us consider
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}
. We have

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}=0}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}-1}\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}\right)\,

(D.38)

Invoking the bracketing definition (Definition
2
), we obtain sets
U_{j}
, for
j=1,\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{B}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}},\varepsilon,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})
such that, for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}-1
, any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}
, any
j
, with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}_{j}(\cdot\mid\cdot)\coloneqq\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\in U_{j}}\density_{#1}(\cdot\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}\cdot,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})
,

\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}_{j}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}})d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\leq 1+\varepsilon\,.

(D.39)

Therefore, for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}
, any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\geq 1
, any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}
,
there exists
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}\in\{1,\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{B}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}},\varepsilon,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})\}
such that

\displaystyle\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}}).

(D.40)

Hence, we can bound

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right)\leq\exp\left(\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}=0}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}-1}\log\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}+\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\log\frac{1+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\right).

(D.41)

We now control the contribution from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}
by simply taking the supremum over this set. We have

\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[\one\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\}\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right)\right]

(D.42)

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\right)\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}}\left(\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}\right)^{1/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\,.

(D.43)

Combining
Eqs.

D.41
and
D.43
, we bound the
LHS
of the statement as

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}\ex_{#1}\left[\one\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\}\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right)\right]\right]

(D.44)

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}\ex_{#1}\left[\one\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\}\log\ex_{#1}\left[\one\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right)\right]+\one\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right)\right]

(D.45)

\displaystyle\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}\ex_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}\bigg[\one\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\}\log\bigg(\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{B}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}},\varepsilon,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})}\exp\left(\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}=0}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}-1}\log\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}+\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\log\frac{1+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\right)

(D.46)

\displaystyle\quad+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\right)\cdot\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}}\left(\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}\right)^{1/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\bigg)\bigg]\,.

(D.47)

Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}\in(0,1)
, for any non-negative numbers
a_{1},\dots,a_{K}
we have
\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k}\right)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}
. Using this inequality and that
\log(a+b)\leq\log(1+a)+\log(1+b)
for
a,b\geq 0
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}\ex_{#1}\left[\one\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\}\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right)\right]\right]

(D.48)

\displaystyle\leq\ex_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}\bigg[\one\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\}\log\bigg(\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{B}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}},\varepsilon,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})}\exp\left(\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}=0}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}-1}\log\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}+{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}\log\frac{1+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\right)

(D.49)

\displaystyle\quad+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\right)^{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}}\cdot\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}}\left(\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}\right)\bigg)\bigg]

(D.50)

\displaystyle\leq\ex_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}\bigg[\one\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\}\log\left(1+\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{B}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}},\varepsilon,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})}\exp\left(\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}=0}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}-1}\log\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}+{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}\log\frac{1+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\right)\right)

(D.51)

\displaystyle\quad+\log\left(1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\right)^{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}}\cdot\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}}\left(\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}\right)\right)\bigg]\,.

(D.52)

Using Jensen’s inequality on the first term, we have

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}\ex_{#1}\left[\one\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\}\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right)\right]\right]

(D.53)

\displaystyle\leq\log\left(1+\ex_{#1}\left[\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{B}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}},\varepsilon,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})}\exp\left(\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}=0}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}-1}\log\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}+{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}\log\frac{1+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\right)\right]\right)

(D.54)

\displaystyle\quad+\ex_{#1}\left[\log\left(1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\right)^{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}}\cdot{\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}}\left(\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}\right)}\right)\right]

(D.55)

\displaystyle\leq\log\left(1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{B}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}},\varepsilon,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})(1+\varepsilon)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}\left(\frac{1+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\right)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}\right)+\ex_{#1}\left[\log\left(1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\right)^{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}}\cdot\ex_{#1}\left[\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}}\left(\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}\right)\right]\right)\right]\,,

(D.56)

where we used the definition of the bracketing number
Definition

2
in the last line.
To obtain the final result, we perform additional manipulations on each term.
For the first term, we use that
\frac{1}{1-x}\leq 1+2x
for
x\in(0,1/2)
so that

\log\left((1+\varepsilon)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}\left(\frac{1+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\right)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}\right)\leq\log\left((1+2\varepsilon)^{3{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}\right)\leq 6{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\varepsilon\,,

(D.57)

so that

\displaystyle\log\left(1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{B}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}},\varepsilon,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})(1+\varepsilon)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}\left(\frac{1+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\right)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\log\left(1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{B}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}},\varepsilon,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})\right)+6{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\varepsilon

(D.58)

\displaystyle\leq 2\log\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{B}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}},\varepsilon,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})\right)+6{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\varepsilon\,.

(D.59)

For the second term, we use that
\log(1+x)\leq x
and distinguish two cases to obtain

\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[\log\left(1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\right)^{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}}\cdot\ex_{#1}\left[\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}}\left(\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}\right)\right]\right)\right]

(D.60)

\displaystyle\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\right)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}+\ex_{#1}\left[\one\left\{\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}}\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}\geq 1\right\}\cdot\log\left(1+{\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}}\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}}\right)\right]\,.

(D.61)

All that is left to do is to deal with the case
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}
. We have, as above,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}\ex_{#1}\left[\one\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\}\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right)\right]\right]\leq\ex_{#1}\left[\one\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\}\log\left(\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta}}\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}\right)\right]\,.

(D.62)

∎

We now leverage
3
to control the different terms of
Lemma

D.3
.

Lemma D.4
.

For
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}\in(0,1)
, under
3
, for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\geq 1
,
it holds that

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}\ex_{#1}\left[\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right)\right]\right]\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\right)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}+\bigoh\left(\log({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})\right)\,,

(D.63)

where the
\bigoh(\cdot)
hides constants that do not depend on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}
.

Proof.

Fix
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}>0
that will be chosen later and take
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\mathbb{B}}}(0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R})
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\overline{\mathbb{B}}}(0,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R})
.
Let us consider a
\delta
-cover of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}
with
\delta>0
that will be chosen later: there are
K
sets
U_{j}
,
j=1,\dots,K
that cover
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}
such that for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{\prime}\in U_{j}
, we have
\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{\prime}\rVert\leq\delta
. By e.g.,
Wainwright 2019
, we can take
K
such that
\log K\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}\log(1+2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}/\delta)
.

3
ensures that the sets
U_{j}
satisfy the bracketing condition of
Definition

2
with
\varepsilon=\exp(\poly({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R})\delta)-1
. Therefore, we have, with this choice of
\varepsilon
,

\log{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{B}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}},\varepsilon,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}\log(1+2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}/\delta)\,.

(D.64)

Using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and
3
, we have that, both

\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[\one\left\{\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}}\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}\geq 1\right\}\cdot\log\left(1+{\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}}\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\poly({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})}{1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}^{1/k}}

(D.65)

\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[\one\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\right\}\cdot\log\left({\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta}}\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\poly({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})}{1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}^{1/k}}\,.

(D.66)

Choose
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}=\poly({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})
so that both
Eqs.

D.65
and
D.66
are
\bigoh(1)
.
Finally, we choose
\delta=(\poly({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}))^{-1}
so that
\varepsilon=\exp(\poly({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R})\delta)-1=\bigoh(1/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})
.
Combining this
Eqs.

D.64
,
D.65
and
D.66
with
Lemma

D.3
concludes the proof.
∎

We can now state our main result for ICL.
As a metric to asses the quality of a given retrieved task
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}
w.r.t. the true task
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*}
, we consider the Rényi divergence
(
Rényi 1961
)
of order
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}\in(0,1)
between the distributions
\density_{#1}(\cdot\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})
and
\density_{#1}(\cdot\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*})
:

\mathrm{D}_{#1}\!({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\,\|\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*})=-\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}(1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho})}\log\ex_{#1}\left[\prod_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}=1}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\left(\tfrac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*})}\right)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}\right]\,.

(D.67)

Theorem D.1
.

Under
3
, for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}\in(0,1)
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\geq 1
,
it holds that, for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\sim\density_{#1}(\cdot\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*})
,

\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[\ex_{#1}\left[\mathrm{D}_{#1}\!({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\,\|\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*})\right]\right]

(D.68)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}{(1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}\log\left(\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left(-\ex_{#1}\left[\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]\right)\right]\right)

(D.69)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}{1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}\frac{{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\density_{#1}(\cdot\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*})\,\|\,\density_{#1}(\cdot\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}))}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}

(D.70)

\displaystyle+\bigoh\left(\frac{\log({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}\right)\,,

(D.71)

where the
\bigoh(\cdot)
hides constants that do not depend on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}
.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of
Proposition

D.1
combined with
Lemma

D.4
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda}=1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}
and bounding
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\right)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\leq 1
.
∎

A few comments are in order.
The first term of
Eq.

D.69
captures how much the prior
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}
covers the reference task
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}
. When
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*}
, this term thus quantifies how well the prior covers the true task
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*}
. When
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}
is inside the support of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}
, this term is vanishing as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}
grows large, see the next results below.

The second term of
Eq.

D.70
captures how well the reference task
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}
approximates the true task
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*}
.
When
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*}
, the term of
Eq.

D.70
is 0. Otherwise, consider the case the KL will typically be of order
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}
so that this term is
\bigoh(1)
: it represents the best ICL error one can hope for when the true task
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*}
is not in the support of the prior
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}
.

D.5
Laplace Approximation

We will make use of the following version of the Laplace approximation, see
Wong 2001
for a proof.

Lemma D.5

(Laplace approximation)
.

Let
\mu
be a probability measure on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with density
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,\infty)
. Fix
x^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
g
is continuous at
x^{*}
and
g(x^{*})>0
.
Then, as
\varepsilon\to 0
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2\varepsilon}\,\|x-x^{*}\|\Bigr)\,g(x)\,dx,\;=\;g(x^{*})\,C\,\varepsilon^{d}\;+\;o(\varepsilon^{d}).

where
C\;\coloneqq\;\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\|y\|\Bigr)\,dy\in(0,\infty)
.

Assumption 4
.

Consider the following additional assumptions to
3
:

•

Tail behaviour:
for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\geq 1
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}>0
,

\displaystyle\prob_{\!#1}\left({\sup_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}:\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert\geq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\geq{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\poly({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})}e^{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}}

(D.72)

\displaystyle\prob_{\!#1}\Big({\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T},\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\rVert\geq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\poly({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})e^{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}}\,.

(D.73)

•

Regularity of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}
:
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}
is continuous and positive at
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}
.

•

Second moment of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}
:

\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert^{2}\right]

\displaystyle<\infty\,.

(D.74)

Proposition D.2
.

Under
3
and
4
, then, for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}
large enough,

\displaystyle-\log\left(\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left(-\ex_{#1}\left[\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]\right)\right]\right)\leq\log 1/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})+\bigoh(\poly(\log{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}))\,.

(D.75)

Proof.

For some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\geq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}>0
, we split the term as

\displaystyle-\log\left(\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left(-\ex_{#1}\left[\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]\right)\right]\right)

(D.76)

\displaystyle=-\log\left(\ex_{#1}\left[\one\left\{\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\right\}\exp\left(-\ex_{#1}\left[\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]\right)+\one\left\{\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert>{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\right\}\exp\left(-\ex_{#1}\left[\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]\right)\right]\right)

(D.77)

\displaystyle\leq-\log\left(\ex_{#1}\left[\one\left\{\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\right\}\exp\left(-\ex_{#1}\left[\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]\right)+\one\left\{\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert>{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\right\}\exp\left(-\ex_{#1}\left[\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]\right)\right]\right)

(D.78)

Using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and
3
and its refinement in the statement, we bound the second term as, for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}
such that
\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert>{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}
,
so that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\ex_{#1}\left[\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]\right\rvert\leq e^{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}/2}\poly({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})\,.

(D.79)

so that

\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[\one\left\{\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert>{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\right\}\exp\left(-\ex_{#1}\left[\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]\right)\right]

(D.80)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(e^{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}/2}\poly({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})\right){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}\left(\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert>{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\right)

(D.81)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(e^{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}/2}\poly({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})\right)\frac{\ex_{#1}\left[\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert^{2}\right]}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}^{2}}\,,

(D.82)

where we used Markov’s inequality in the last line. Take
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}^{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}+1)}/2
so that
Eq.

D.82
is
\bigoh(1/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}+1})
.

We now focus on the first term of
Eq.

D.78
and bound it as:

\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]

\displaystyle=\ex_{#1}\left[\one\left\{\max_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\rVert\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\right\}\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]+\ex_{#1}\left[\one\left\{\max_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\rVert>{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\right\}\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]

(D.83)

\displaystyle\leq\poly({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})T\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}\rVert+\poly({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})e^{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}/2}

(D.84)

where we used the regularity assumption of
3
for the first term and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality combined with
4
for the second term.

Take
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}=\poly(\log{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})
so that
\poly({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})e^{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}/2}=\bigoh(1)
and assume that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}
is large enough so that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\geq\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}\rVert+1
.

Putting everything together, we have

\displaystyle-\log\left(\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left(-\ex_{#1}\left[\log\frac{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{\density_{#1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]\right)\right]\right)

(D.85)

\displaystyle\leq-\log\left(\ex_{#1}\left[\one\left\{\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\right\}\exp\left(-\poly({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}\rVert+\bigoh(1)\right)+\bigoh\left(\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}+1}}\right)\right]\right)

(D.86)

\displaystyle\leq-\log\left(\ex_{#1}\left[\one\left\{\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert\leq\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}\rVert+1\right\}\exp\left(-\poly(\log{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}\rVert+\bigoh(1)\right)+\bigoh\left(\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}+1}}\right)\right]\right)\,,

(D.87)

where we used that we assumed that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}=\poly(\log{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})\geq\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}\rVert+1
.

Applying
Lemma

D.5
with
\varepsilon=1/(\poly(\log{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})
yields:

\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[\one\left\{\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert\leq\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}\rVert+1\right\}\exp\left(-\poly(\log{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}\rVert\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\poly(\log{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}^{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})C+o(1)\right)\,,

(D.88)

where
C
is the constant of
Lemma

D.5
and this concludes the proof.

∎

We can now combine
Theorems

D.1
and
D.2
to obtain the final result in the main text.

Theorem D.2
.

Under
3
and
4
, for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}\in(0,1)
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\geq 1
,
it holds that, for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}}\sim\density_{#1}(\cdot\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*})
,

\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[\ex_{#1}\left[\mathrm{D}_{#1}\!({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\,\|\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*})\right]\right]

(D.89)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}{(1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}\log 1/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})

(D.90)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}{1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}\frac{{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\density_{#1}(\cdot\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*})\,\|\,\density_{#1}(\cdot\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}))}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}

(D.91)

\displaystyle+\bigoh\left(\frac{\log({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T})}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}\right)\,,

(D.92)

where the
\bigoh(\cdot)
hides constants that do not depend on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}
.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of
Theorems

D.1
and
D.2
.
∎

Appendix E
Generalization bounds

E.1
Moment bounds for general functions

In this subsection, we generalize the heavy-tail concentration results of
Li and Liu 2024a
to allow for non-i.i.d. data.
This section can also be seen as extending concentration results for dependent sequences to the case where the function of interest does not necessarily admit bounded differences but only bounded moments.
In particular,
Lemma

E.1
extends the coupling argument of
Chazottes et al. 2007
to our setting, in particular not requiring bounded differences but only bounded moments. Indeed, for this, we replace the total variation distance by the Wasserstein-1 distance. It can also be seen as an extension of the bounded differences result of
Kontorovich and Ramanan 2008
to our setting (see
Mohri and Rostamizadeh 2010
for a presentation of the results of
Kontorovich and Ramanan 2008
in a setting closer to ours).
Moreover, note that even the handling of the subGaussian increments is much more trickier than in
Kontorovich 2014
, since we have to carefully apply a convex domination argument to handle the conditional dependence.
The main result of this section is
Theorem

E.1
, which is of independent interest.

As in the previous section,
\lVert\cdot\rVert
denotes the Euclidean norm on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}^{d}
for any
d\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}
.

At multiple places, we will use the Wasserstein-1 distance
§
§

§

This is a slight abuse of terminology, since the Wasserstein-1 distance is usually defined for metric spaces, while we only assume
\rho
to be a cost function. However, this slight abuse of terminology will not cause any confusion in the following.

with respect to a cost function
\rho\colon\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\to[0,\infty)
, defined as

W_{#1}(\mu,\nu):=\inf_{\pi\in\Pi(\mu,\nu)}\int\rho(z,z^{\prime})d\pi(z,z^{\prime}),

(E.1)

where
\Pi(\mu,\nu)
is the set of couplings of
\mu
and
\nu
. We refer to the textbook
Villani 2008
for more details.

Lemma E.1
.

Consider
\mathcal{Z}
measurable space. Let
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m}
be
\mathcal{Z}
-valued random variables with natural filtration
\mathcal{F}_{i}:=\sigma(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{i})
. For each
i
, assume there is
Z_{i}^{\prime}
such that

Z_{i}^{\prime}\sim\text{Law}(Z_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}),\quad Z_{i}^{\prime}\perp\!\!\!\perp Z_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}.

(E.2)

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}\colon\mathcal{Z}^{m}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}
be measurable and coordinate-wise Lipschitz with respect to cost functions
\rho_{i}\colon\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\to[0,\infty)
such that
\rho_{i}(z_{i},z_{i})=0
,
with constants
L_{i}\geq 0
: for any
z,z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}^{m}
differing only in the
i
-th coordinate,

\lvert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(z)-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(z^{\prime})\rvert\leq L_{i}\rho_{i}(z_{i},z_{i}^{\prime}).

(E.3)

With
W_{#1}(\cdot,\cdot)
the Wasserstein-1 distance with respect to
\rho_{j}
, define, for
i<j
,

\delta_{i,j}(z_{1:i},z_{i}^{\prime})=W_{#1}(\text{Law}(Z_{j}\mid Z_{1:i}=z_{1:i}),\text{Law}(Z_{j}\mid Z_{1:i-1}=z_{1:i-1},Z_{i}=z_{i}^{\prime})).

(E.4)

for
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
,

\lvert\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1:m})\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{i}]-\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1:i-1},Z_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i+1:m})\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{i-1},Z_{i}^{\prime}]\rvert\leq L_{i}\rho_{i}(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime})+\sum_{j=i+1}^{m}L_{j}\delta_{i,j}(Z_{1:i},Z_{i}^{\prime})

(E.5)

Proof.

Fix
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
. We condition on
\mathcal{F}_{i-1}
. Let
u:=Z_{i}
and
u^{\prime}:=Z_{i}^{\prime}
.
Not to overburden notations, all expectations and probabilities in the following are conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{i-1},Z_{i}=u,Z_{i}^{\prime}=u^{\prime}
.
Define the tail functions

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}(z_{i+1:m})

\displaystyle:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1:(i-1)},u,z_{i+1:m}),

(E.6)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(z_{i+1:m})

\displaystyle:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1:(i-1)},u^{\prime},z_{i+1:m}).

(E.7)

Denote
Z_{(i+1):m}\sim\text{Law}(Z_{(i+1):m}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1},Z_{i}=u)
and

Z_{(i+1):m}^{\prime}\sim\text{Law}(Z_{(i+1):m}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1},Z_{i}=u^{\prime})
. We decompose

\displaystyle\lvert\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1:m})]-\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1:(i-1)},Z_{i:m}^{\prime})]\rvert

(E.8)

\displaystyle=\big|\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}(Z_{(i+1):m})]-\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m}^{\prime})]\big|

(E.9)

\displaystyle\leq\ex\left[\big|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}(Z_{(i+1):m})-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m})\big|\right]+\Big|\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m})\right]-\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m}^{\prime})\right]\Big|.

(E.10)

We bound the two terms separately.

By the coordinate-wise Lipschitz condition at
i
,

\ex_{#1}\left[|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}(Z_{(i+1):m})-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m})|\right]\leq L_{i}\rho_{i}(u,u^{\prime})=L_{i}\rho_{i}(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime}).

(E.11)

We write the following telescoping decomposition:

\displaystyle\Big|\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m})\right]-\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m}^{\prime})\right]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=i}^{m-1}\Big|\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):j}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})\right]-\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):(j+1)}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})\right]\Big|\,.

(E.12)

By the definition of the Wasserstein-1 distance, there exists a coupling of
(Z_{j+1},Z_{j+1}^{\prime})
such that

\ex\left[\rho_{j+1}(Z_{j+1},Z_{j+1}^{\prime})\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime}\right]=W_{#1}(\text{Law}(Z_{j+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i}),\text{Law}(Z_{j+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1},Z_{i}^{\prime}))\leq\delta_{i,j+1}(Z_{1:i-1},Z_{i}^{\prime})\,.

(E.13)

We obtain a bound on the increment at coordinate
j
by combining the coupling with the coordinate-wise Lipschitz condition at
j
:

\displaystyle\Big|\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):j}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})\right]-\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):(j+1)}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})\right]\Big|

(E.14)

\displaystyle\leq\ex\left[\big|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):j}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):(j+1)}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})\big|\right]

(E.15)

\displaystyle\leq L_{j+1}\ex\left[\rho_{j+1}(Z_{j+1},Z_{j+1}^{\prime})\right]

(E.16)

\displaystyle=L_{j+1}W_{#1}(\text{Law}(Z_{j+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i}),\text{Law}(Z_{j+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1},Z_{i}^{\prime}))=L_{j+1}\delta_{i,j+1}(Z_{1:i},Z_{i}^{\prime})\,.

(E.17)

Combining the above estimates gives

\displaystyle\Big|\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m})\right]-\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m}^{\prime})\right]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=i}^{m-1}L_{j+1}\delta_{i,j+1}(Z_{1:i},Z_{i}^{\prime})\,.

(E.18)

which yields the desired result.
∎

We now state a classic convex domination lemma which is a slight variant of
Ledoux and Talagrand 2013
.

Lemma E.2

(Convex domination)
.

Consider
X,Z
a zero-mean symmetric random variables such that

\prob(|X|>t)\leq C\prob(|Z|>t)\,,

(E.19)

for some
C>0
and all
t>0
.

Then, for any convex function
h\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}
,

\ex[h(X)]\leq\ex[h(CZ)]\,.

(E.20)

Proof.

Let
\delta\sim\mathrm{Bernoulli}(1/C)
be independent of
(X,Z)
. Then, for all
t>0
,

\prob(|Z|>t)\geq\tfrac{1}{C}\,\prob(|X|>t)=\prob(|\delta X|>t).

Hence
|\delta X|
is stochastically dominated by
|Z|
and we may construct a coupling such that

|\delta X|\leq|Z|\qquad\text{a.s.}

(E.21)

Since
X
is symmetric, we may write in distribution
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\,|X|
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}
is a Rademacher variable independent of
|X|
. Likewise,
Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}^{\prime}\,|Z|
with an independent Rademacher
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}^{\prime}
.

Condition on
(\delta,X,Z)
and define

\Phi(a)\;:=\;\ex\big[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\!\big(a\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\,|Z|\big)\,\bigm|\,\delta,X,Z\big]\,,\qquad a\in[-1,1].

(E.22)

The map
a\mapsto\Phi(a)
is convex (as an average of convex functions). By convexity, its maximum on
[-1,1]
is attained at an extreme point
\{-1,1\}
. On the coupling where (
E.21
) holds, define

a\;:=\;\begin{cases}\dfrac{\delta\lvert X\rvert}{|Z|},&\text{if }Z\neq 0,\\[6.0pt]
0,&\text{if }Z=0,\end{cases}

(E.23)

so that
a\in[-1,1]
almost surely thanks to
|X|\leq|\delta Z|
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\ex\big[\,h\!\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\,|X|\,\delta\big)\,\bigm|\,\delta,X,Z\big]\;=\;\Phi(a)\;\leq\;\max\{\Phi(-1),\Phi(1)\}\;=\;\ex\big[\,h\!\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\,|Z|\big)\,\bigm|\,\delta,|X|,Z\big].

(E.24)

Taking expectations and using
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}|X|
and
Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}|Z|
,

\ex[h\!\big(\delta X\big)]\;\leq\;\ex[h(Z)].

(E.25)

Since
h
is convex and
\ex[\delta\mid X,Z]=1/C
, we have, by Jensen’s inequality,

\ex[h\!\big(X/C\big)]\;=\;\ex\big[\,h\!\big(\ex[\delta X\mid X,Z]\big)]\;\leq\;\ex\big[\,\ex[h(\delta X)\mid X,Z]\,]\;=\;\ex[h(\delta X)]\leq\ex[h(Z)]\,,

(E.26)

Finally, apply the previous inequality with the convex function
u\mapsto h(Cu)
to obtain

\ex[h(X)]\;=\;\ex[h\!\big(C\cdot(X/C)\big)]\;\leq\;\ex[h\!\big(CZ\big)].

This is exactly the desired bound.

∎

We now state a fact of subGaussian random variables, which can be found in
Wainwright 2019
for instance.

Lemma E.3

(Convex domination)
.

Consider
X
a zero-mean real-valued
\sigma^{2}
-sub-Gaussian random variable,
which is, in addition, symmetric, i.e.,
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}-X
.
Then, for
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
,

\prob(|X|>t)\leq 8\prob(|Z|>t)\,.

(E.27)

Lemma E.4

(Causal symmetrization)
.

Let
m\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}
and
(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{A})
be a standard Borel measurable space. Let
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m}
be
\mathcal{Z}
-valued random with natural filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i=0,\dots,m}

Let
h\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}
be convex.

Consider
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}\colon\mathcal{Z}^{m}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}
be measurable. Set
S:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m})
.
For each
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
, assume there exists a conditionally independent resample

Z_{i}^{\prime}\sim\mathrm{Law}(Z_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}),\quad Z_{i}^{\prime}\perp\!\!\!\perp Z_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}.

(E.28)

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{1:m},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{1:m}^{\prime}
be independent Rademacher variables, independent of all
Z,Z^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}_{m}
.

Assume there exist measurable functions
c_{i}\colon\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\to[0,\infty)
,
d_{i}:\mathcal{Z}\to[0,\infty)
and
J\subset\left\{1,\ldots,m\right\}
such that, the following conditions hold:

(i)

For any
i
, there exists
j(i)\in J
, such that, for any
z_{1:i-1}\in\mathcal{Z}^{i-1}
and
z_{i},z_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}
,

\left\lvert\ex\left[S\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:i}=z_{1:i}\right]-\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:i-1}=z_{1:i-1},Z_{i}=z_{i}^{\prime}]\right\rvert\leq c_{i}(z_{i},z_{i}^{\prime})+d_{i}(z_{j(i)})\one\{i\not\in J\}\,.

(E.29)

(ii)

For any
i\notin J
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}c_{i}(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime})
is
\sigma_{i}^{2}
-sub-Gaussian conditionally on
\mathcal{F}_{i-1}
.

(iii)

For any
j\in J
,
Z_{j}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{j-1}
.

Then, there are Gausssian random variables
G_{j},G_{j}^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,8\sigma_{j}^{2})
independent and independent of all
Z,Z^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon},\mathcal{F}_{m}
such that

\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}(S-\ex[S])]\leq\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(\sum_{i\notin J}\sym_{#1}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{j(i)})\right)\right)+\sum_{j\in J}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{j}c_{j}(Z_{j},Z_{j}^{\prime})\right)\right],

(E.30)

where we use the notation:

\sym_{#1}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{j(i)})\right)\right)\coloneqq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{j(i)}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{j(i)})\right)-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}(\lvert G_{i}^{\prime}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{j(i)}^{\prime}))\right)\,.

(E.31)

Proof.

Define
\mathcal{G}=\sigma({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{1:m},G_{1:m})
.

We show the result by induction on
k
: our goal is to show that, for any
k\in\{0,\ldots,m\}
,

\displaystyle\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}(S-\ex[S])]

\displaystyle\leq\ex\biggl[h\Bigl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\notin J\\
i\geq k+1\end{subarray}}\bigl(\one\{j(i)\leq k\}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{j(i)})\right)+\one\{j(i)\geq k+1\}\sym_{#1}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{j(i)})\right)\right)\bigr)

(E.32)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\Bigl.+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in J\\
i\geq k+1\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}c_{i}(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime})+\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k}]-\ex[S]\Bigr)\biggr]\,,

(E.33)

where
G_{i},G_{i}^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,8\sigma_{i}^{2})
are independent and independent of all
Z,Z^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}_{m}
.

Equation

E.33
holds trivially for
k=m
. We now show that if it holds for some
k\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
, then it also holds for
k-1
.

Note that we can rewrite

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\notin J\\
i\geq k+1\end{subarray}}\bigl(\one\{j(i)\leq k\}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{j(i)})\right)+\one\{j(i)\geq k+1\}\sym_{#1}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{j(i)})\right)\right)\bigr)

(E.34)

\displaystyle\quad\Bigl.+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in J\\
i\geq k+1\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}c_{i}(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime})

(E.35)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\notin J\\
i\geq k+1\end{subarray}}\one\{j(i)\geq k+1\}\sym_{#1}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{j(i)})\right)\right)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in J\\
i\geq k+1\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}c_{i}(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime})}_{=:Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}}

(E.36)

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\notin J\\
i\geq k+1\end{subarray}}\one\{j(i)\leq k\}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{j(i)})\right)}_{=:Y_{k}}

(E.37)

\displaystyle=Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}+Y_{k}\,,

(E.38)

where
Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{k}
and
Y_{k}
is
\mathcal{F}_{k}
-measurable.
More precisely, we show that

\displaystyle\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}+Y_{k}+\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k}]-\ex[S]\right)\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right]

(E.39)

\displaystyle\leq\ex\left[h\left(Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}+Y_{k-1}+\one\{k\notin J\}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{k}(\lvert G_{k}\rvert+d_{k}(Z_{j(k)}))\right.\right.

(E.40)

\displaystyle\quad+\one\{k\in J\}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{k}c_{k}(Z_{k},Z_{k}^{\prime})

(E.41)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\notin J\\
i\geq k+1\\
j(i)=k\end{subarray}}\sym_{#1}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{k})\right)\right))\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}]-\ex[S])|Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}],,

(E.42)

with
Y_{k-1}:=\sum_{i\notin J,i\geq k+1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\one\{j(i)\leq k-1\}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{j(i)})\right)
,
which will imply the induction step
Eq.

E.33
with
k\leftarrow k-1

by taking expectations over
Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}
.
Since
Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}
is considered constant in
Eq.

E.42
, we may assume without loss of generality that
Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}=0
, at the potential cost of replacing
h
by
h(\cdot+Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp})
, which is still convex.
Therefore, it suffices to show

\displaystyle\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(Y_{k}+\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k}]-\ex[S]\right)\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right]

(E.43)

\displaystyle\leq\ex\left[h\left(Y_{k-1}+\one\{k\notin J\}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{k}(\lvert G_{k}\rvert+d_{k}(Z_{j(k)}))\right.\right.

(E.44)

\displaystyle\quad+\one\{k\in J\}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{k}c_{k}(Z_{k},Z_{k}^{\prime})

(E.45)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\notin J\\
i\geq k+1\\
j(i)=k\end{subarray}}\sym_{#1}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{k})\right)\right))\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}]-\ex[S])|Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}],,

(E.46)

We first consider the case of
k\notin J
.
Define
\Phi(z_{1:k}):=\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k}=z_{1:k}]
. We rewrite the
RHS
of
Eq.

E.46
as

\displaystyle\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(Y_{k}+\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k}]-\ex[S]\right)\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right]

(E.47)

\displaystyle=\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(Y_{k}+\Phi(Z_{1:k})-\ex\left[\Phi(Z_{1:k-1},Z_{k}^{\prime})\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}\right]+\ex\left[S\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}\right]-\ex[S]\right)|Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right]

(E.48)

\displaystyle=\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(Y_{k}+\ex\left[\Phi(Z_{1:k})-\Phi(Z_{1:k-1},Z_{k}^{\prime})\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k}\right]+\ex\left[S\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}\right]-\ex[S]\right)|Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right]

(E.49)

\displaystyle=\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(Y_{k}+\ex\left[\Phi(Z_{1:k})-\Phi(Z_{1:k-1},Z_{k}^{\prime})\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k},\mathcal{G}\right]+\ex\left[S\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}\right]-\ex[S]\right)|Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right]

(E.50)

where we used the fact that
\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}]=\ex[\Phi(Z_{1:k-1},Z_{k}^{\prime})\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}]=\ex[\Phi(Z_{1:k_{1}},Z_{k}^{\prime})\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k}]=\ex[\Phi(Z_{1:k-1},Z_{k}^{\prime})\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k},\mathcal{G}]
,
since
Z_{k}^{\prime}\sim\text{Law}(Z_{k}\mid Z_{1:k-1})
and
Z_{k}^{\prime}\perp\!\!\!\perp Z_{k}\mid Z_{1:k-1}
and
\mathcal{G}
is independent of all
Z,Z^{\prime}
.
Since both
Y_{k}
and
\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}]-\ex[S]
are
\sigma(\mathcal{F}_{k},\mathcal{G})
-measurable, by Jensen’s inequality (convexity of
h
) applied to the conditional expectation w.r.t.
Z_{1:k},\mathcal{G}
, we have

\displaystyle\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(Y_{k}+\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k}]-\ex[S]\right)\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right]

(E.52)

\displaystyle\leq\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(Y_{k}+\Phi(Z_{1:k})-\Phi(Z_{1:k-1},Z_{k}^{\prime})+\ex\left[S\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}\right]-\ex[S]\right)\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right]\,.

(E.53)

Since
k\not\in J
, then
Y_{k}
is
\sigma(\mathcal{F}_{k-1},\mathcal{G})
-measurable. The following argument will now be made conditionally on
\mathcal{F}_{k-1},\mathcal{G},Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}
.

We have that
\Phi(Z_{1:k})-\Phi(Z_{1:k-1},Z_{k}^{\prime})
is symmetric. Moreover, since
\lvert\Phi(Z_{1:k})-\Phi(Z_{1:k-1},Z_{k}^{\prime})\rvert\leq c_{k}(Z_{k},Z_{k}^{\prime})+d_{k}(Z_{j(k)}))
by assumption (i), we have that, for any
t>0
,

\displaystyle\prob\left(|\Phi(Z_{1:k})-\Phi(Z_{1:k-1},Z_{k}^{\prime})|>t\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{k-1},\mathcal{G},Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right)

(E.54)

\displaystyle\leq\prob\left(c_{k}(Z_{k},Z_{k}^{\prime})+d_{k}(Z_{j(k)})>t\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{k-1},\mathcal{G},Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right)

(E.55)

\displaystyle\leq\prob\left(c_{k}(Z_{k},Z_{k}^{\prime})>t-d_{k}(Z_{j(k)})\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{k-1},\mathcal{G},Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right)

(E.56)

\displaystyle\leq 8\prob\left(|G_{k}|>t-d_{k}(Z_{j(k)})\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{k-1},\mathcal{G},Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right)\,,

(E.57)

where we used that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{k}c_{k}(Z_{k},Z_{k}^{\prime})
is
\sigma_{k}^{2}
-sub-Gaussian conditionally on
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
by assumption (ii) and
Lemma

E.3
.
Therefore, we can apply
Lemma

E.2
with
X\leftarrow\Phi(Z_{1:k})-\Phi(Z_{1:k-1},Z_{k}^{\prime})
and
Z\leftarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{k}(|G_{k}|+d_{k}(Z_{j(k)}))
with
C=8
conditionally on
\mathcal{F}_{k-1},Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}
to obtain

\displaystyle\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(Y_{k}+\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k}]-\ex[S]\right)\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right]

(E.58)

\displaystyle\leq\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(Y_{k}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{k}(|G_{k}|+d_{k}(Z_{j(k)}))+\ex\left[S\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}\right]-\ex[S]\right)\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right]\,,

(E.59)

which is
Eq.

E.46
in the case
k\notin J
.

For the case
k\in J
, we use a similar argument.
We now have, as before,

\displaystyle\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}]

\displaystyle=\ex[\Phi(Z_{1:k-1},Z_{k}^{\prime})\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}]

(E.60)

\displaystyle=\ex[\Phi(Z_{1:k-1},Z_{k}^{\prime})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\notin J\\
i\geq k+1\\
j(i)=k\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\left(\lvert G_{i}^{\prime}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{k})\right)\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}]

(E.61)

\displaystyle=\ex[\Phi(Z_{1:k-1},Z_{k}^{\prime})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\notin J\\
i\geq k+1\\
j(i)=k\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\left(\lvert G_{i}^{\prime}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{k})\right)\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k},\mathcal{G}]\,,

(E.62)

by construction.

Since both
Y_{k}
and
\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}]-\ex[S]
are
\sigma(\mathcal{F}_{k},\mathcal{G})
-measurable,
by Jensen’s inequality (convexity of
h
) applied to the conditional expectation w.r.t.
Z_{1:k},\mathcal{G}
, we have

\displaystyle\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(Y_{k}+\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k}]-\ex[S]\right)\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right]

(E.63)

\displaystyle\leq\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(Y_{k}+\Phi(Z_{1:k})-\Phi(Z_{1:k-1},Z_{k}^{\prime})-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\notin J\\
i\geq k+1\\
j(i)=k\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\left(\lvert G_{i}^{\prime}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{k})\right)+\ex\left[S\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}\right]-\ex[S]\right)\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right]\,.

(E.64)

We write
Y_{k}
as

Y_{k}=Y_{k-1}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\notin J\\
i\geq k+1\\
j(i)=k\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{k})\right)\,,

(E.65)

where
Y_{k-1}
is
\sigma(\mathcal{F}_{k-1},\mathcal{G})
-measurable and obtain,

\displaystyle\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(Y_{k-1}+\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k}]-\ex[S]\right)\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right]

(E.66)

\displaystyle\leq\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(Y_{k-1}+\Phi(Z_{1:k})-\Phi(Z_{1:k-1},Z_{k}^{\prime})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\notin J\\
i\geq k+1\\
j(i)=k\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{k})\right)-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\left(\lvert G_{i}^{\prime}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{k})\right)+\ex\left[S\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:k-1}\right]-\ex[S]\right)\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}\right]\,.

(E.67)

We now make the following domination argument conditionally on
\mathcal{F}_{k-1},Y_{k-1},Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}
.
The random variable

\Phi(Z_{1:k})-\Phi(Z_{1:k-1},Z_{k}^{\prime})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\notin J\\
i\geq k+1\\
j(i)=k\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{k})\right)-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\left(\lvert G_{i}^{\prime}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{k})\right)

(E.68)

is symmetric and, by assumption (i) and the triangle inequality, bounded in absolute value by

\left\lvert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{k}c_{k}(Z_{k},Z_{k}^{\prime})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\notin J\\
i\geq k+1\\
j(i)=k\end{subarray}}\sym_{#1}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{k})\right)\right)\right\rvert\,.

(E.69)

Applying
Lemma

E.2
conditionally on
\mathcal{F}_{k-1},Y_{k-1},Y_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\perp\!\!\!\perp}
with
C=1
(hence no constant appears) yields the desired result.

∎

We can now combine
Lemma

E.1
and
Lemma

E.4
to obtain the main moment bound of this section.

Theorem E.1

(Causal symmetrization)
.

Let
m\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}
and
(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{A})
be a standard Borel measurable space. Let
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m}
be
\mathcal{Z}
-valued random with natural filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i=0,\dots,m}
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}
be convex.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}\colon\mathcal{Z}^{m}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}
be measurable and coordinate-wise Lipschitz with respect to cost functions
\rho_{i}\colon\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\to[0,\infty)
such that
\rho_{i}(z_{i},z_{i})=0
with constants
L_{i}\geq 0
: for any
z,z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}^{m}
differing only in the
i
-th coordinate,

|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(z)-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(z^{\prime})|\leq L_{i}\rho_{i}(z_{i},z_{i}^{\prime}).

(E.70)

Set
S:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m})
and

For each
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
, assume there exists a conditionally independent resample

Z_{i}^{\prime}\sim\mathrm{Law}(Z_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}),\quad Z_{i}^{\prime}\perp\!\!\!\perp Z_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}.

(E.71)

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{1:m},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{1:m}^{\prime}
be independent Rademacher variables, independent of all
Z,Z^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}_{m}
.

Assume there exist constants
c_{ik}\geq 0
, measurable functions
d_{ik}:\mathcal{Z}\to[0,\infty)
and
J\subset\left\{1,\ldots,m\right\}
such that, the following conditions hold:

(i)

For any
i<k
, there exists
j(i)\in J
, such that, for any
z_{1:i-1}\in\mathcal{Z}^{i-1}
and
z_{i},z_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}
,

W_{#1}\left(\mathrm{Law}(Z_{k}\mid Z_{1:i}=z_{1:i}),\mathrm{Law}(Z_{k}\mid Z_{1:i-1}=z_{1:i-1},Z_{i}=z_{i}^{\prime})\right)\leq c_{ik}\rho_{i}(z_{i},z_{i}^{\prime})+d_{ik}(z_{j(i)})\one\{i\notin J\}\,.

(E.72)

(ii)

For any
i\notin J
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\rho_{i}(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime})
is
\sigma_{i}^{2}
-sub-Gaussian conditionally on
\mathcal{F}_{i-1}
.

(iii)

For any
j\in J
,
Z_{j}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{j-1}
.

Then, there are Gausssian random variables
G_{j},G_{j}^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,8\sigma_{j}^{2})
independent and independent of all
Z,Z^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon},\mathcal{F}_{m}
such that

\displaystyle\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}(S-\ex[S])]

(E.73)

\displaystyle\leq\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(\sum_{i\notin J}\sym_{#1}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(L_{i}\lvert G_{i}\rvert+\sum_{k>i}L_{k}c_{ik}\lvert G_{i}\rvert+L_{k}d_{ik}(Z_{j(i)})\right)\right)+\sum_{j\in J}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{j}\left(L_{j}\rho_{j}(Z_{j},Z_{j}^{\prime})+\sum_{k>j}L_{k}c_{jk}\rho_{j}(Z_{j},Z_{j}^{\prime})\right)\right)\right],

(E.74)

where we use the notation:

\displaystyle\sym_{#1}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(L_{i}\lvert G_{i}\rvert+\sum_{k>i}L_{k}c_{ik}\lvert G_{i}\rvert+L_{k}d_{ik}(Z_{j(i)})\right)\right)\coloneqq

(E.75)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{j(i)}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(L_{i}\lvert G_{i}\rvert+\sum_{k>i}L_{k}c_{ik}\lvert G_{i}\rvert+L_{k}d_{ik}(Z_{j(i)})\right)-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}\left(L_{i}\lvert G_{i}^{\prime}\rvert+\sum_{k>i}L_{k}c_{ik}\lvert G_{i}^{\prime}\rvert+L_{k}d_{ik}(Z_{j(i)})\right)\right)\,.

(E.76)

E.2
Technical lemmas

We will make use of the following elementary lemma.

Lemma E.5
.

Let
Z
be a real-valued random variable. Assume there exist
c\geq 1
,
f,g\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}_{+}
non-decreasing and
p\geq 2
integer such that, for any integer
q\in[2,p]
,

\ex[|Z|^{q}]^{1/q}\leq f(q)+c^{1/q}g(q)

(E.77)

Then, for any
\delta\in(0,e^{-2}]
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\left\lvert Z\right\rvert\leq\begin{cases}ef\left(\log(1/\delta)+1\right)+g(\log(1/\delta)+1)e&\text{if }\delta\geq ce^{-p}\\
\frac{f(p)+c^{1/p}g(p)}{\delta^{1/p}}&\text{if }\delta<ce^{-p}\,.\end{cases}

(E.78)

Proof.

By Markov’s inequality, for any integer
q\in[2,p]
,

\prob(|Z|\geq t)\leq\frac{\ex[|Z|^{q}]}{t^{q}}\leq\left(\frac{f(q)+c^{1/q}g(q)}{t}\right)^{q}\,.

(E.79)

Setting the right-hand side to
\delta
and solving for
t
gives

t=\frac{f(q)+c^{1/q}g(q)}{\delta^{1/q}}\,,

(E.80)

If
\delta<ce^{-p}
, we can take
q=p
to obtain the second case of the result. If
\delta\geq ce^{-p}
, we take
q
the smallest integer such that
q\geq\log(c/\delta)
. Note that
q
is in
[2,p]
and
q\leq\log(c/\delta)+2
.

Since
c\geq 1
and
\delta\leq 1
, we have
\log(c/\delta)\geq 0
and thus
\left(\frac{c}{\delta}\right)^{1/q}\leq\left(\frac{c}{\delta}\right)^{1/\log(c/\delta)}=e
.
Plugging this into
Eq.

E.80
gives the bound in the first case.
∎

We state the following lemma about sub-Gaussian random variables that will be useful later.

Lemma E.6
.

Let
X\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}^{m}
be a
\sigma^{2}
-sub-Gaussian random variable, i.e., for any
\lambda>0
,

\log\ex[e^{\lambda\lVert X-\ex[X]\rVert^{2}}]\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}\,.

(E.81)

Then, for
X^{\prime}
an
iid
copy of
X
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}
a Rademacher random variable independent of
X,X^{\prime}
, the random variable
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\lVert X-X^{\prime}\rVert
is sub-Gaussian with parameter at most
4\sigma^{2}
.

Proof.

Since
Z\coloneqq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\lVert X-X^{\prime}\rVert
is symmetric, it suffices to bound
Z^{2}
as

Z^{2}=\lVert X-X^{\prime}\rVert^{2}\leq 2\lVert X-\ex[X]\rVert^{2}+2\lVert X^{\prime}-\ex[X]\rVert^{2}\,,

(E.82)

by Young’s inequality. Using the independence of
X
and
X^{\prime}
yields the result.
∎

We will require the following chaining lemma for processes with
L^{p}
-Lipschitz increments.
This result is a variant of the famous Dudley’s entropy integral bound for sub-Gaussian processes, adapted to the
L^{p}
-Lipschitz setting.

This lemma is a direct consequence of the general chaining theory of
Talagrand 2022
(see
Talagrand 2022
with
\phi(x)=x^{p}
). Let us also mention
Dirksen 2015
refined these ideas in the context of subpexponential processes while
Latała and Tkocz 2015
further developed these tools for processes with heavier tails but still admitting a control over all moments. In our setting, the increments are assumed to be controlled only in
L^{p}
, which requires a different treatment of the maximal inequalities at each scale.

Lemma E.7

(Dudley–type entropy integral under
L^{p}
increments)
.

Let
(X_{t})_{t\in T}
be a real-valued process indexed by a pseudometric space
(T,d)
.
Assume
T
is totally bounded with diameter
\Delta:=\mathrm{diam}_{d}(T)\in(0,\infty)
and that for some
p>1
and
L>0
,

\|X_{t}-X_{s}\|_{p}\;\leq\;L\,d(t,s)\qquad\forall\,s,t\in T.

(E.83)

Then

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\sup_{s,t\in T}\bigl(X_{t}-X_{s}\bigr)\right]\;\leq\;C\,L\int_{0}^{\Delta}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}(T,d,\varepsilon)\bigr)^{1/p}\,d\varepsilon,

(E.84)

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}(T,d,\varepsilon)
is the
\varepsilon
-covering number and
C<\infty
is an absolute constant.

E.3
Concentration bounds for
icl
(
icl
)

We now apply the moment symmetrization results to derive concentration bounds for
icl
in the dependent data setting.
These concentration bounds will then be translated into generalization bounds in the next subsection.

Let us recall ICL notations.

We denote by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta}\subset{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}
the space of tasks
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}
and by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})
the density of the pretraining task distribution.
Given a task
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}
, the data is generated according to a task-specific distribution with density
\density(\cdot\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})
.
The training data is then generated by first sampling a task
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}
from the task distribution
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}
, and then sampling data points
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\geq 1}
according to

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}+1}\sim\density_{#1}(\cdot\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})\,.

(E.85)

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}})
.

Given a dataset of tasks
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{1},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}
and associated samples
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}^{(1)},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}^{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N})}
, a model
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}}
is trained by minimizing the next-sample prediction loss

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\widehat{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}},({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n}})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}})=\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n}=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}=1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ell}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}-1}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}n})\,,

(E.86)

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ell}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}\times{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}\to[0,+\infty)
is a loss function at step
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}
.

We now provide a detailed version of
2
.

Assumption 5

(Weak dependence)
.

We assume that there are deterministic coefficients
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\geq 1}
and
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\geq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\geq 1}
such that, for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\geq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\geq 1
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta}
, any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}-1)}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}-1}
, and any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}
,

\displaystyle W_{#1}(\density_{#1}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}),\density_{#1}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}^{\prime}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{\prime}\rVert

(E.87)

\displaystyle W_{#1}(\density_{#1}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}),\density_{#1}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}^{\prime}\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{1:({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}-1)},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}))

\displaystyle\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}B}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}}\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert\,.

(E.88)
